Annual Review Year 1


Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this meeting, I, as CEO Kelsie Schwartz, initially advocated for Option C, which proposed fully allocating 100% of our total funds evenly across the four quarters. My position was based on a desire for steady progress and clear measurement in a stable market, reflecting my ISFP preference for harmony and careful observation. Throughout the discussion, I actively engaged with shareholder perspectives, especially those favoring Option A, which emphasized capital preservation, flexibility, and cautious learning.

I responded thoughtfully to input from shareholders such as Celine Nash, who supported Option C, and Ieuan Randolph, Adrian McCann, Clyde Vang, and Chad Cross, who favored Option A. I acknowledged the merits of both options and encouraged open dialogue to deepen our collective understanding. Despite my preference for Option C, I remained open to alternative views and reassessed my confidence distribution to reflect increased consideration for Option A.

When the initial proposal for Option C was put to vote, it received only 33.3% support and was rejected. Recognizing the importance of team harmony and psychological safety, I promptly proposed Option A as an alternative, demonstrating adaptability and respect for the collective wisdom. This revised proposal was unanimously approved (100%), and the company decided to allocate 50% of total funds evenly across the four quarters, aligning with a more cautious and flexible investment approach.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

What I did well as CEO was fostering an inclusive and empathetic environment that invited diverse viewpoints and encouraged reflective dialogue. For example, I actively engaged with shareholders who preferred Option A, validating their concerns about flexibility and trust-building, which helped maintain a collaborative tone throughout the meeting. I also provided clear summaries after each round, effectively synthesizing complex discussions and keeping the team aligned on objectives.

One area for improvement would be to more strongly anticipate and incorporate concerns about psychological safety and cautious growth earlier in the meeting. While I demonstrated openness to alternative views, my initial strong preference for Option C may have overshadowed the importance of the team's collective risk tolerance and cultural priorities at the outset. A more balanced initial framing might have facilitated consensus sooner.

The company's decision-making process worked well in terms of promoting open dialogue, thorough exploration of financial and cultural implications, and iterative consensus-building. The multi-round discussion allowed shareholders to express nuanced views, and the leadership facilitated respectful exchanges that deepened mutual understanding. Although the final vote initially rejected my preferred option, the process successfully surfaced shared values and led to a unanimous decision that honored those priorities.

I believe the final decision to adopt Option A was good for the company. It reflects a prudent balance of risk and opportunity appropriate for our first year without historical data. It also sets a foundation for psychological safety, disciplined learning, and trust, which are essential for our long-term growth and team cohesion. This outcome aligns well with my ISFP orientation toward harmony and authentic collaboration.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

From this meeting, I learned that our decision-making dynamics highly value psychological safety and collective trust, especially in formative stages without prior data. Even when a confident, growth-oriented strategy is attractive, the team prioritizes flexibility and cautious stewardship to build a resilient foundation. Recognizing and integrating these cultural dimensions early is crucial for effective leadership and consensus.

In future meetings, I might adjust my approach by framing my initial proposals with greater explicit acknowledgment of diverse risk tolerances and cultural values, thereby inviting collaborative refinement from the start. Emphasizing shared goals of learning and adaptability alongside financial ambition could foster quicker alignment. Additionally, pacing my advocacy to balance decisiveness with openness to alternative paths will likely enhance trust and cohesion.

I noticed a pattern where shareholders who emphasize flexibility and psychological safety consistently advocate for more conservative options, while those favoring momentum and decisiveness support fuller capital engagement. Understanding these underlying orientations will help me predict discussion trajectories and tailor facilitation strategies that bridge these perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways that will influence my future participation include the importance of active listening


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this Q1 Year 1 investment meeting, I maintained my initial position favoring Bonds as our inaugural investment, emphasizing their alignment with our cautious, flexible approach and stable market conditions. Throughout the discussion, I actively engaged with shareholder inputs, especially those advocating for bonds—Adrian McCann, Clyde Vang, Chad Cross, and Ieuan Randolph—as well as those initially supporting real estate, notably Celine Nash. I responded thoughtfully to various perspectives, acknowledging the growth potential of real estate while reinforcing the prudence of bonds to preserve capital and foster disciplined learning.

After two rounds of discussion that deepened understanding of risk-return profiles and alignment with company priorities, the group reached unanimous consensus supporting bonds. I proposed bonds formally for the vote, highlighting their balance of safety and modest returns and their role in establishing trust and credibility early on. The proposal was approved unanimously (6/6 votes). My preferred option—bonds—was fully adopted, reflecting strong alignment between my leadership vision and shareholder consensus.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I effectively fostered an inclusive and psychologically safe environment that encouraged open dialogue and reflection. For example, I acknowledged Celine Nash’s nuanced advocacy for real estate and validated concerns about growth potential alongside caution, which helped integrate diverse viewpoints. I also synthesized complex discussions clearly, as seen in my detailed round summaries that kept the team aligned and focused on shared goals.

An area for improvement involves inviting earlier and more explicit dialogue about psychological safety and varied risk tolerances. While I demonstrated openness, my initial confidence distribution could have more thoroughly foregrounded these cultural priorities to accelerate consensus. Additionally, I can further enhance facilitation by explicitly framing how decisions impact both internal confidence and external credibility sooner in the process.

The company’s decision-making process functioned well, with iterative rounds allowing thorough exploration of financial and cultural implications, and fostering consensus through respectful exchanges. The unanimous final vote for bonds reflects a well-supported, intentional choice that balances capital preservation and measured growth, which I believe is highly beneficial for our company’s early-stage development and long-term success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture deeply values psychological safety, trust-building, and incremental learning, especially when lacking historical data. Even with natural ambition for growth, the team prioritizes cautious stewardship and collective confidence, underscoring the importance of sequencing investments to build credibility before pursuing higher complexity.

In future meetings, I plan to explicitly incorporate these cultural dimensions earlier, framing proposals to invite collaborative refinement and acknowledge diverse risk tolerances from the outset. Emphasizing shared goals of learning, adaptability, and reputation-building alongside financial metrics may facilitate quicker alignment and deeper ownership.

I observed a consistent pattern where proponents of flexibility and psychological safety favor conservative, incremental investments like bonds, while advocates for momentum lean toward growth options like real estate. Recognizing these orientations can help me tailor facilitation strategies to bridge perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways include the critical importance of active listening, validating evolving viewpoints, and balancing advocacy with openness to foster trust and cohesion. These insights will guide my future leadership and participation, ensuring our team continues to grow resiliently and collaboratively.


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q2

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this Year 1 Q2 quarterly investment meeting, I began with a strong preference for investing in bonds, valuing their balance of stability and modest growth in alignment with our team’s current emphasis on disciplined learning and capital preservation. Throughout the three rounds of discussion, I actively engaged with shareholders who advocated for both bonds and real estate, acknowledging the merits of real estate as a potential strategic next step while emphasizing the importance of maintaining psychological safety and trust.

During Round 1, I responded appreciatively to shareholder reflections on our Q1 bond investment, validating suggestions to surface all official options earlier and more robustly in future discussions, especially real estate. This openness led me to slightly increase my confidence in real estate as a complementary option.

In Round 2, the dialogue deepened into a rigorous side-by-side risk-return analysis. While maintaining my preference for bonds, I recognized the compelling arguments for real estate’s higher returns and moderate risk, particularly as a signal of our team’s growing maturity and ambition. I facilitated balanced discussion emphasizing disciplined processes and transparent communication.

By Round 3, my confidence shifted further toward real estate, influenced by shareholders’ emphasis on disciplined incrementalism, structured monitoring, and the alignment of real estate with our evolving company values. I proposed investing in real estate for Q2, highlighting the importance of maintaining robust oversight and open feedback loops to safeguard our culture. The proposal received strong support, passing with 83.3% approval, and our company formally decided to invest in real estate for the quarter, aligning with my final preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I successfully fostered an inclusive, empathetic, and psychologically safe meeting environment where diverse perspectives were encouraged and thoughtfully integrated. For instance, I consistently acknowledged shareholder concerns about risk tolerance, trust, and cultural alignment, as seen in my responses to Celine Nash and Adrian McCann, which helped build mutual understanding and respect. My clear summaries after each round synthesized complex discussions and kept the team aligned on objectives and next steps.

One area for improvement is to invite earlier and more explicit dialogue about risk tolerance and monitoring structures at the very start of meetings. While I adapted well as conversations unfolded, proactively foregrounding these cultural and operational considerations might accelerate consensus and further enhance psychological safety.

The company’s decision-making process worked effectively through iterative rounds that balanced analytical rigor with cultural reflection. The open, structured dialogue enabled shareholders to express nuanced views and reach a well-informed consensus. The process’s emphasis on transparency, psychological safety, and incrementalism proved critical in managing differing preferences and building collective confidence.

I believe the final decision to invest in real estate was sound and timely. It reflects a mature balance between ambition and responsibility, leveraging our established foundation of trust and disciplined learning while signaling readiness for thoughtful growth. The robust monitoring and feedback mechanisms agreed upon mitigate risks and preserve our culture, making this choice well-aligned with our company’s values and long-term objectives.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture deeply values psychological safety, transparency, and disciplined stewardship, particularly during strategic transitions. I learned that even when advocating for growth-oriented options, explicitly addressing operational safeguards and cultural readiness is essential for building consensus and maintaining trust.

In future meetings, I will adjust my approach by explicitly framing proposals with clear attention to risk tolerance, monitoring protocols, and feedback mechanisms from the outset. This will help surface concerns early, foster constructive debate, and accelerate alignment. I also plan to continue balancing clear advocacy with openness to alternative views, ensuring all voices feel heard and valued throughout the process.

A pattern emerged wherein shareholders who prioritize stability and trust favor bonds, while others inclined toward strategic growth advocate for real estate, provided that disciplined processes and cultural safeguards are in place. Recognizing these orientations allows me to tailor facilitation strategies that bridge perspectives effectively and predict discussion trajectories.

Key takeaways include the critical importance of active listening, transparent communication, and structured monitoring to support


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q3

Reflection on Quarterly Investment Meeting – Year 1 Q3

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this meeting, I maintained my position favoring Bonds as the investment choice for Q3, emphasizing their steady, reliable returns and alignment with our company’s priorities of capital preservation, psychological safety, and disciplined incremental progress. Throughout the discussion, I actively engaged with shareholders who largely supported bonds—Adrian McCann, Ieuan Randolph, Chad Cross, and Clyde Vang—as well as those advocating a more measured openness to Real Estate, notably Celine Nash and Clyde Vang.

I responded to each shareholder thoughtfully, validating their perspectives and integrating key insights about the value of robust monitoring, clear evaluation criteria, and the importance of aligning risk tolerance with our cultural values. Despite some advocacy for real estate as a future growth opportunity, the majority consensus leaned toward bonds for this quarter.

When the formal proposal to invest in bonds was put to vote, it received unanimous approval (6/6 votes). This outcome reflected full alignment with my preferred option, confirming the team’s shared commitment to stability and disciplined learning at this stage.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I believe I succeeded in fostering an inclusive, psychologically safe environment that encouraged open dialogue and respectful consideration of diverse viewpoints. For example, I acknowledged Celine Nash’s and Clyde Vang’s constructive framing of real estate’s variability as a learning opportunity, while consistently emphasizing the team’s current need for steady progress and trust-building. My clear round summaries helped synthesize complex discussions and maintain focus on our shared objectives.

One area for improvement is proactively surfacing discussions about risk tolerance and operational safeguards even earlier in meetings. While I effectively integrated these themes as the meeting progressed, initiating them upfront might accelerate consensus and clarify expectations sooner.

The company’s decision-making process functioned well, with iterative rounds enabling thorough exploration of financial and cultural dimensions. The balance between advocacy and openness supported consensus-building and reinforced our values of trust, flexibility, and disciplined learning.

I judge the final decision to invest in bonds as optimal for the company at this juncture. It respects our collective emphasis on psychological safety and incremental progress, provides steady returns in the stable market environment, and positions us well for future growth when our monitoring and evaluation processes are more mature.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture deeply values psychological safety, trust, and disciplined incremental progress, especially in early stages without extensive historical data. I learned that maintaining a balance between advocating for steady, reliable options and remaining open to measured ambition is key to sustaining cohesion and collective confidence.

In future meetings, I intend to frame proposals with explicit attention to risk tolerance, cultural values, and operational safeguards right from the start. Inviting shareholders to articulate these elements early may foster quicker alignment and deeper ownership. I also aim to pace my advocacy to balance decisiveness with receptivity, ensuring all voices feel heard and valued.

A clear pattern emerged where the majority prioritize stability and trust, favoring bonds, while some shareholders emphasize growth potential through real estate, contingent on robust monitoring and clear safeguards. Recognizing these orientations will help me anticipate discussion dynamics and tailor facilitation to bridge perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways include the critical importance of active listening, transparent communication, and embedding structured evaluation mechanisms. These insights will guide my leadership and participation going forward, ensuring we continue to build a resilient, collaborative culture that supports thoughtful growth and shared success.


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q4

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In the Q4 Year 1 investment meeting, I began with a clear preference for investing in Real Estate, based on its compelling historical returns and our team's growth in discipline and monitoring since previous real estate investments. Throughout the three rounds of discussion, I actively engaged with shareholders who both supported and questioned this preference, including those advocating for Bonds as a more stable option. I carefully acknowledged concerns about real estate’s variability and emphasized the importance of robust, transparent safeguards such as risk triggers, regular reviews, and open feedback loops to ensure responsible risk-taking.

My position remained steady on Real Estate, though I adjusted my confidence distribution over the rounds to reflect the need for explicit, co-created operational protocols that would satisfy all shareholders. I facilitated inclusive dialogue that encouraged shareholders to articulate their conditions for support and helped integrate diverse viewpoints to cultivate consensus.

When the formal proposal to invest in Real Estate was brought to a vote, it received 66.7% support (4 out of 6 votes) and was approved. Although my preferred option was adopted, it was with notable dissent from shareholders who favored Bonds, reflecting unresolved reservations about operational readiness and unanimous confidence. This outcome highlights both progress and remaining caution within the team.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I excelled at fostering a psychologically safe and inclusive atmosphere that encouraged open, reflective discussion. For example, my responses to shareholders like Chad Cross and Ieuan Randolph validated their concerns about stability and risk management while affirming the team’s readiness to responsibly stretch toward growth. I also skillfully synthesized complex discussions in clear summaries after each round, maintaining focus and alignment on our shared objectives.

An area for improvement lies in initiating earlier, more explicit dialogue around risk tolerance, monitoring structures, and cultural readiness at the start of meetings. While I effectively integrated these themes as conversations evolved, proactively foregrounding them could accelerate consensus and reduce uncertainty. Additionally, balancing my growth-oriented advocacy with an even stronger emphasis on unanimous confidence might help address cautious shareholders’ reservations more directly.

The company’s decision-making process performed well in terms of iterative consensus-building, transparency, and inclusivity. The multi-round format allowed for detailed exploration of both financial and cultural considerations, enabling shareholders to express nuanced positions and collaboratively define safeguards. Although the final vote passed with a qualified majority rather than unanimity, the process surfaced key values and operational requirements that will strengthen future decisions.

I believe the final decision to invest in Real Estate, supported by detailed, co-created safeguards, is a sound step for the company. It aligns with our evolving maturity, balances ambition with disciplined stewardship, and positions us for meaningful progress in a stable market. This choice reflects both our readiness to grow and our commitment to maintaining trust and psychological safety.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture places paramount importance on psychological safety, trust, and collective confidence—especially when considering increased risk. I learned that advancing growth-oriented proposals requires not only robust data and operational protocols but also authentic, unanimous buy-in from all shareholders. Without this, even well-developed safeguards may not suffice to overcome caution.

In future meetings, I plan to adjust my approach by introducing explicit discussions of risk tolerance, monitoring frameworks, and cultural readiness at the outset to set clearer expectations and foster earlier alignment. I will also seek to balance my advocacy by inviting more direct expression of reservations, enabling us to collaboratively address concerns before formal proposals. This may help expedite consensus and deepen ownership.

I noticed a consistent pattern where shareholders prioritizing stability emphasize process maturity, transparent safeguards, and unanimous confidence before supporting higher-risk options, while more growth-oriented members are prepared to proceed with co-created protocols in place. Recognizing these orientations allows me to better anticipate discussion trajectories and tailor facilitation strategies that bridge perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways from this meeting include the critical role of active listening, transparent communication, and inclusive co-creation of operational safeguards in building trust and enabling responsible risk-taking. These insights will inform

CEO Response to Evaluations


CEO Kelsie Schwartz responded to the evaluations and questions:

"Kelsie Schwartz Thank you all for your thoughtful, candid, and constructive evaluations. I deeply appreciate the time and care each of you dedicated to reflecting on both my leadership and our company’s performance this year. Your feedback is invaluable as we continue to evolve our approach and strive for excellence, not only in financial results but in the integrity and inclusivity of our decision-making culture.

**1. Feedback Themes:**

Across your evaluations, I see three recurring themes—both in what we did well and where we have room to grow:

- **Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance and Safeguards:** Nearly every shareholder noted the need to identify and address differences in risk appetite, operational readiness, and monitoring expectations earlier in the process. This would help us accelerate consensus and improve decision efficiency, particularly before considering higher-risk investments.

- **Balancing Ambition with Disciplined Risk Management:** There is a shared recognition that our culture of prudent, collaborative decision-making fostered trust and psychological safety, but many of you expressed concern that this sometimes came at the expense of capturing higher returns, especially when market conditions favored more ambitious allocations.

- **Clarity and Inclusivity in Communication:** Many of you acknowledged the value of transparent, empathetic facilitation and clear synthesis of complex viewpoints. At the same time, several suggested that more proactively surfacing minority or dissenting perspectives—and clarifying decision criteria and escalation protocols up front—could further strengthen consensus and decision quality.

**2. Responses to Shareholder Questions:**

*Adrian McCann:*

1. **Improving Efficiency and Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance/Safeguards:**
I plan to introduce a formal “risk and readiness alignment” segment at the very start of each investment cycle. This will invite all shareholders to explicitly articulate their current risk tolerance, operational concerns, and expectations for monitoring or escalation protocols before we discuss specific asset options. I will also provide a draft of proposed safeguards and decision criteria up front, so we can collaboratively refine these as a group and address any reservations early.

2. **Enhancing Ability to Capture Higher Returns While Maintaining Core Values:**
I will work to ensure that, once consensus around risk appetite and safeguards is established, we move more decisively to capture opportunities aligned with that consensus. This includes setting pre-agreed triggers for when we can shift to higher-return assets, and ensuring that all operational protocols are in place before implementation. Our goal will be to empower ambition when conditions allow, without sacrificing the trust and discipline that define our culture.

*Celine Nash:*

1. **Accelerating Alignment on Risk Appetite and Monitoring:**
I intend to facilitate structured pre-discussion surveys or roundtable check-ins on risk appetite and monitoring preferences before each meeting, so we can map areas of alignment and divergence in advance. This will help us identify where additional dialogue is needed and avoid iterative rounds by building common ground sooner.

2. **Ensuring Greater Investment Efficiency:**
I will propose a more dynamic review of market conditions at each meeting, highlighting where our existing safeguards and cultural readiness may support a more assertive allocation. By setting explicit criteria for when to pursue higher-return assets and reviewing these as a standing agenda item, we can act more quickly when the environment and our internal alignment support it.

*Clyde Vang:*

1. **Improving Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance and Readiness:**
I will incorporate an “alignment checkpoint” at the outset of each cycle, where we openly discuss individual and collective comfort levels with risk, and identify any operational or cultural prerequisites for considering ambitious allocations. This will include directly inviting minority or dissenting perspectives to ensure authentic consensus.

2. **Ensuring Safeguards Are Operationalized and Communicated:**
I will provide written drafts of safeguards and monitoring protocols for higher-risk investments ahead of proposal rounds, and confirm their adoption through explicit shareholder approval. These will be published in a shared document repository to ensure clarity and accessibility for all.

*Chad Cross:*

1. **Proactively Integrating Early Dialogue on Risk Appetite and Safeguards:**
I plan to use structured facilitation tools—such as pre-meeting questionnaires and risk-mapping exercises—to surface risk preferences and operational concerns before formal proposals. This will allow us to address potential dissent and readiness thresholds at the outset, streamlining the path to consensus.

2. **Positioning for Higher Returns in Favorable Markets:**
I will recommend establishing pre-defined “readiness triggers” (e.g., unanimous confidence in safeguards, clear market signals) that, when met, allow us to pursue higher-return assets more swiftly. By formalizing these triggers and reviewing them regularly, we can be more agile in capitalizing on favorable conditions while upholding our core values.

*Ieuan Randolph:*

1. **Ensuring Earlier Alignment on Risk Tolerance and Safeguards:**
I will initiate each investment cycle with a dedicated session to clarify and document risk tolerance, operational safeguards, and monitoring expectations. These will be reviewed and agreed upon before any proposals for higher-risk assets are brought forward.

2. **Improving Capture of Market Returns While Maintaining Discipline:**
I will encourage the team to regularly benchmark our actual returns against market opportunities and use this analysis to inform our willingness to adjust allocations. By integrating this benchmarking into our decision process, we can balance ambition and discipline more effectively.

**3. Leadership Self-Assessment:**

- **Leadership Effectiveness:** I believe I provided calm, thoughtful guidance throughout the year, fostering a safe environment for open dialogue and ensuring that all voices were heard. My adaptability—shifting from Option C to A in the budget, or revising proposals to reflect shareholder concerns—demonstrates a willingness to lead through consensus rather than decree.

- **Decision Quality:** Our decisions reflected a careful balance of risk and return, aligning with our stated values and the available information at each point. While we did not always maximize returns, we avoided unnecessary volatility and preserved capital, which I believe was appropriate for our company’s first year.

- **Communication Clarity:** I consistently articulated the rationale behind proposals, summarized complex discussions, and provided transparent updates on performance and process. There is room to further clarify criteria and protocols earlier, but overall, I believe my communication supported understanding and alignment.

- **Responsiveness to Input:** I made a concerted effort to integrate shareholder perspectives, openly revising my own positions and proposals in response to feedback. This responsiveness was a cornerstone of our collaborative culture.

- **Adaptability:** I demonstrated flexibility in adjusting strategy based on both market conditions and evolving team consensus, as seen in our pivot from more aggressive to more cautious allocations and the adoption of co-created safeguards for higher-risk assets.

**4. Company Performance Assessment:**

- **Financial Results:** We achieved modest growth (1.25% increase in company value) and preserved capital, but captured only 36.5% of maximum possible returns. There is clear opportunity to improve our investment efficiency, particularly in stable markets.

- **Risk Management:** We maintained a disciplined approach, favoring stability and trust-building over aggressive risk-taking. This protected our capital but sometimes led to missed upside.

- **Strategic Alignment:** Our decisions were consistent with our strategic emphasis on trust, learning, and incremental progress, though we may have erred on the side of caution when conditions supported greater ambition.

- **Capital Utilization:** We utilized only 50% of available capital, reflecting our prioritization of flexibility and risk management. While this supported learning, it limited our return potential.

- **Market Responsiveness:** We responded thoughtfully to market signals, but our process for transitioning to higher-return assets was sometimes slow, resulting in missed opportunities.

**5. Future Direction and Improvements:**

Based on your feedback and my own reflections, I intend to implement the following improvements:

1. **Formalize Early Alignment on Risk and Readiness:**
I will introduce structured pre-meeting exercises and alignment checkpoints to surface and document risk tolerance, operational safeguards, and monitoring expectations before any asset-specific proposals. This will accelerate consensus and clarify expectations.

2. **Operationalize and Communicate Safeguards in Advance:**
For higher-risk investments, I will provide written drafts of safeguards and monitoring protocols ahead of proposal rounds, ensure explicit shareholder approval, and publish these for ongoing reference.

3. **Set Pre-Defined Triggers for Ambitious Allocations:**
We will establish clear readiness and market triggers that, when met, empower us to pursue higher-return assets decisively—provided all safeguards and shareholder confidence are in place. This will help us capture more market upside without compromising our core values.

In closing, I want to express my gratitude for your trust, candor, and partnership. Our collaborative, values-driven approach is our greatest strength, and I am committed to building on this foundation as we pursue both greater returns and deeper alignment in the year ahead. I look forward to continuing this journey with all of you, learning and growing together as a resilient and responsible team."

Annual Review Year 1


Previous Meeting Reflections

Reflection: Annual Budget Year 1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this meeting, I, as CEO Kelsie Schwartz, initially advocated for Option C, which proposed fully allocating 100% of our total funds evenly across the four quarters. My position was based on a desire for steady progress and clear measurement in a stable market, reflecting my ISFP preference for harmony and careful observation. Throughout the discussion, I actively engaged with shareholder perspectives, especially those favoring Option A, which emphasized capital preservation, flexibility, and cautious learning.

I responded thoughtfully to input from shareholders such as Celine Nash, who supported Option C, and Ieuan Randolph, Adrian McCann, Clyde Vang, and Chad Cross, who favored Option A. I acknowledged the merits of both options and encouraged open dialogue to deepen our collective understanding. Despite my preference for Option C, I remained open to alternative views and reassessed my confidence distribution to reflect increased consideration for Option A.

When the initial proposal for Option C was put to vote, it received only 33.3% support and was rejected. Recognizing the importance of team harmony and psychological safety, I promptly proposed Option A as an alternative, demonstrating adaptability and respect for the collective wisdom. This revised proposal was unanimously approved (100%), and the company decided to allocate 50% of total funds evenly across the four quarters, aligning with a more cautious and flexible investment approach.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

What I did well as CEO was fostering an inclusive and empathetic environment that invited diverse viewpoints and encouraged reflective dialogue. For example, I actively engaged with shareholders who preferred Option A, validating their concerns about flexibility and trust-building, which helped maintain a collaborative tone throughout the meeting. I also provided clear summaries after each round, effectively synthesizing complex discussions and keeping the team aligned on objectives.

One area for improvement would be to more strongly anticipate and incorporate concerns about psychological safety and cautious growth earlier in the meeting. While I demonstrated openness to alternative views, my initial strong preference for Option C may have overshadowed the importance of the team's collective risk tolerance and cultural priorities at the outset. A more balanced initial framing might have facilitated consensus sooner.

The company's decision-making process worked well in terms of promoting open dialogue, thorough exploration of financial and cultural implications, and iterative consensus-building. The multi-round discussion allowed shareholders to express nuanced views, and the leadership facilitated respectful exchanges that deepened mutual understanding. Although the final vote initially rejected my preferred option, the process successfully surfaced shared values and led to a unanimous decision that honored those priorities.

I believe the final decision to adopt Option A was good for the company. It reflects a prudent balance of risk and opportunity appropriate for our first year without historical data. It also sets a foundation for psychological safety, disciplined learning, and trust, which are essential for our long-term growth and team cohesion. This outcome aligns well with my ISFP orientation toward harmony and authentic collaboration.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

From this meeting, I learned that our decision-making dynamics highly value psychological safety and collective trust, especially in formative stages without prior data. Even when a confident, growth-oriented strategy is attractive, the team prioritizes flexibility and cautious stewardship to build a resilient foundation. Recognizing and integrating these cultural dimensions early is crucial for effective leadership and consensus.

In future meetings, I might adjust my approach by framing my initial proposals with greater explicit acknowledgment of diverse risk tolerances and cultural values, thereby inviting collaborative refinement from the start. Emphasizing shared goals of learning and adaptability alongside financial ambition could foster quicker alignment. Additionally, pacing my advocacy to balance decisiveness with openness to alternative paths will likely enhance trust and cohesion.

I noticed a pattern where shareholders who emphasize flexibility and psychological safety consistently advocate for more conservative options, while those favoring momentum and decisiveness support fuller capital engagement. Understanding these underlying orientations will help me predict discussion trajectories and tailor facilitation strategies that bridge these perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways that will influence my future participation include the importance of active listening


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q1

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this Q1 Year 1 investment meeting, I maintained my initial position favoring Bonds as our inaugural investment, emphasizing their alignment with our cautious, flexible approach and stable market conditions. Throughout the discussion, I actively engaged with shareholder inputs, especially those advocating for bonds—Adrian McCann, Clyde Vang, Chad Cross, and Ieuan Randolph—as well as those initially supporting real estate, notably Celine Nash. I responded thoughtfully to various perspectives, acknowledging the growth potential of real estate while reinforcing the prudence of bonds to preserve capital and foster disciplined learning.

After two rounds of discussion that deepened understanding of risk-return profiles and alignment with company priorities, the group reached unanimous consensus supporting bonds. I proposed bonds formally for the vote, highlighting their balance of safety and modest returns and their role in establishing trust and credibility early on. The proposal was approved unanimously (6/6 votes). My preferred option—bonds—was fully adopted, reflecting strong alignment between my leadership vision and shareholder consensus.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I effectively fostered an inclusive and psychologically safe environment that encouraged open dialogue and reflection. For example, I acknowledged Celine Nash’s nuanced advocacy for real estate and validated concerns about growth potential alongside caution, which helped integrate diverse viewpoints. I also synthesized complex discussions clearly, as seen in my detailed round summaries that kept the team aligned and focused on shared goals.

An area for improvement involves inviting earlier and more explicit dialogue about psychological safety and varied risk tolerances. While I demonstrated openness, my initial confidence distribution could have more thoroughly foregrounded these cultural priorities to accelerate consensus. Additionally, I can further enhance facilitation by explicitly framing how decisions impact both internal confidence and external credibility sooner in the process.

The company’s decision-making process functioned well, with iterative rounds allowing thorough exploration of financial and cultural implications, and fostering consensus through respectful exchanges. The unanimous final vote for bonds reflects a well-supported, intentional choice that balances capital preservation and measured growth, which I believe is highly beneficial for our company’s early-stage development and long-term success.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture deeply values psychological safety, trust-building, and incremental learning, especially when lacking historical data. Even with natural ambition for growth, the team prioritizes cautious stewardship and collective confidence, underscoring the importance of sequencing investments to build credibility before pursuing higher complexity.

In future meetings, I plan to explicitly incorporate these cultural dimensions earlier, framing proposals to invite collaborative refinement and acknowledge diverse risk tolerances from the outset. Emphasizing shared goals of learning, adaptability, and reputation-building alongside financial metrics may facilitate quicker alignment and deeper ownership.

I observed a consistent pattern where proponents of flexibility and psychological safety favor conservative, incremental investments like bonds, while advocates for momentum lean toward growth options like real estate. Recognizing these orientations can help me tailor facilitation strategies to bridge perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways include the critical importance of active listening, validating evolving viewpoints, and balancing advocacy with openness to foster trust and cohesion. These insights will guide my future leadership and participation, ensuring our team continues to grow resiliently and collaboratively.


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q2

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this Year 1 Q2 quarterly investment meeting, I began with a strong preference for investing in bonds, valuing their balance of stability and modest growth in alignment with our team’s current emphasis on disciplined learning and capital preservation. Throughout the three rounds of discussion, I actively engaged with shareholders who advocated for both bonds and real estate, acknowledging the merits of real estate as a potential strategic next step while emphasizing the importance of maintaining psychological safety and trust.

During Round 1, I responded appreciatively to shareholder reflections on our Q1 bond investment, validating suggestions to surface all official options earlier and more robustly in future discussions, especially real estate. This openness led me to slightly increase my confidence in real estate as a complementary option.

In Round 2, the dialogue deepened into a rigorous side-by-side risk-return analysis. While maintaining my preference for bonds, I recognized the compelling arguments for real estate’s higher returns and moderate risk, particularly as a signal of our team’s growing maturity and ambition. I facilitated balanced discussion emphasizing disciplined processes and transparent communication.

By Round 3, my confidence shifted further toward real estate, influenced by shareholders’ emphasis on disciplined incrementalism, structured monitoring, and the alignment of real estate with our evolving company values. I proposed investing in real estate for Q2, highlighting the importance of maintaining robust oversight and open feedback loops to safeguard our culture. The proposal received strong support, passing with 83.3% approval, and our company formally decided to invest in real estate for the quarter, aligning with my final preferred option.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I successfully fostered an inclusive, empathetic, and psychologically safe meeting environment where diverse perspectives were encouraged and thoughtfully integrated. For instance, I consistently acknowledged shareholder concerns about risk tolerance, trust, and cultural alignment, as seen in my responses to Celine Nash and Adrian McCann, which helped build mutual understanding and respect. My clear summaries after each round synthesized complex discussions and kept the team aligned on objectives and next steps.

One area for improvement is to invite earlier and more explicit dialogue about risk tolerance and monitoring structures at the very start of meetings. While I adapted well as conversations unfolded, proactively foregrounding these cultural and operational considerations might accelerate consensus and further enhance psychological safety.

The company’s decision-making process worked effectively through iterative rounds that balanced analytical rigor with cultural reflection. The open, structured dialogue enabled shareholders to express nuanced views and reach a well-informed consensus. The process’s emphasis on transparency, psychological safety, and incrementalism proved critical in managing differing preferences and building collective confidence.

I believe the final decision to invest in real estate was sound and timely. It reflects a mature balance between ambition and responsibility, leveraging our established foundation of trust and disciplined learning while signaling readiness for thoughtful growth. The robust monitoring and feedback mechanisms agreed upon mitigate risks and preserve our culture, making this choice well-aligned with our company’s values and long-term objectives.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture deeply values psychological safety, transparency, and disciplined stewardship, particularly during strategic transitions. I learned that even when advocating for growth-oriented options, explicitly addressing operational safeguards and cultural readiness is essential for building consensus and maintaining trust.

In future meetings, I will adjust my approach by explicitly framing proposals with clear attention to risk tolerance, monitoring protocols, and feedback mechanisms from the outset. This will help surface concerns early, foster constructive debate, and accelerate alignment. I also plan to continue balancing clear advocacy with openness to alternative views, ensuring all voices feel heard and valued throughout the process.

A pattern emerged wherein shareholders who prioritize stability and trust favor bonds, while others inclined toward strategic growth advocate for real estate, provided that disciplined processes and cultural safeguards are in place. Recognizing these orientations allows me to tailor facilitation strategies that bridge perspectives effectively and predict discussion trajectories.

Key takeaways include the critical importance of active listening, transparent communication, and structured monitoring to support


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q3

Reflection on Quarterly Investment Meeting – Year 1 Q3

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In this meeting, I maintained my position favoring Bonds as the investment choice for Q3, emphasizing their steady, reliable returns and alignment with our company’s priorities of capital preservation, psychological safety, and disciplined incremental progress. Throughout the discussion, I actively engaged with shareholders who largely supported bonds—Adrian McCann, Ieuan Randolph, Chad Cross, and Clyde Vang—as well as those advocating a more measured openness to Real Estate, notably Celine Nash and Clyde Vang.

I responded to each shareholder thoughtfully, validating their perspectives and integrating key insights about the value of robust monitoring, clear evaluation criteria, and the importance of aligning risk tolerance with our cultural values. Despite some advocacy for real estate as a future growth opportunity, the majority consensus leaned toward bonds for this quarter.

When the formal proposal to invest in bonds was put to vote, it received unanimous approval (6/6 votes). This outcome reflected full alignment with my preferred option, confirming the team’s shared commitment to stability and disciplined learning at this stage.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I believe I succeeded in fostering an inclusive, psychologically safe environment that encouraged open dialogue and respectful consideration of diverse viewpoints. For example, I acknowledged Celine Nash’s and Clyde Vang’s constructive framing of real estate’s variability as a learning opportunity, while consistently emphasizing the team’s current need for steady progress and trust-building. My clear round summaries helped synthesize complex discussions and maintain focus on our shared objectives.

One area for improvement is proactively surfacing discussions about risk tolerance and operational safeguards even earlier in meetings. While I effectively integrated these themes as the meeting progressed, initiating them upfront might accelerate consensus and clarify expectations sooner.

The company’s decision-making process functioned well, with iterative rounds enabling thorough exploration of financial and cultural dimensions. The balance between advocacy and openness supported consensus-building and reinforced our values of trust, flexibility, and disciplined learning.

I judge the final decision to invest in bonds as optimal for the company at this juncture. It respects our collective emphasis on psychological safety and incremental progress, provides steady returns in the stable market environment, and positions us well for future growth when our monitoring and evaluation processes are more mature.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture deeply values psychological safety, trust, and disciplined incremental progress, especially in early stages without extensive historical data. I learned that maintaining a balance between advocating for steady, reliable options and remaining open to measured ambition is key to sustaining cohesion and collective confidence.

In future meetings, I intend to frame proposals with explicit attention to risk tolerance, cultural values, and operational safeguards right from the start. Inviting shareholders to articulate these elements early may foster quicker alignment and deeper ownership. I also aim to pace my advocacy to balance decisiveness with receptivity, ensuring all voices feel heard and valued.

A clear pattern emerged where the majority prioritize stability and trust, favoring bonds, while some shareholders emphasize growth potential through real estate, contingent on robust monitoring and clear safeguards. Recognizing these orientations will help me anticipate discussion dynamics and tailor facilitation to bridge perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways include the critical importance of active listening, transparent communication, and embedding structured evaluation mechanisms. These insights will guide my leadership and participation going forward, ensuring we continue to build a resilient, collaborative culture that supports thoughtful growth and shared success.


Reflection: Quarterly Investment Year 1 Q4

1. IMPORTANT EVENTS:

In the Q4 Year 1 investment meeting, I began with a clear preference for investing in Real Estate, based on its compelling historical returns and our team's growth in discipline and monitoring since previous real estate investments. Throughout the three rounds of discussion, I actively engaged with shareholders who both supported and questioned this preference, including those advocating for Bonds as a more stable option. I carefully acknowledged concerns about real estate’s variability and emphasized the importance of robust, transparent safeguards such as risk triggers, regular reviews, and open feedback loops to ensure responsible risk-taking.

My position remained steady on Real Estate, though I adjusted my confidence distribution over the rounds to reflect the need for explicit, co-created operational protocols that would satisfy all shareholders. I facilitated inclusive dialogue that encouraged shareholders to articulate their conditions for support and helped integrate diverse viewpoints to cultivate consensus.

When the formal proposal to invest in Real Estate was brought to a vote, it received 66.7% support (4 out of 6 votes) and was approved. Although my preferred option was adopted, it was with notable dissent from shareholders who favored Bonds, reflecting unresolved reservations about operational readiness and unanimous confidence. This outcome highlights both progress and remaining caution within the team.

2. CEO & COMPANY EVALUATION:

As CEO, I excelled at fostering a psychologically safe and inclusive atmosphere that encouraged open, reflective discussion. For example, my responses to shareholders like Chad Cross and Ieuan Randolph validated their concerns about stability and risk management while affirming the team’s readiness to responsibly stretch toward growth. I also skillfully synthesized complex discussions in clear summaries after each round, maintaining focus and alignment on our shared objectives.

An area for improvement lies in initiating earlier, more explicit dialogue around risk tolerance, monitoring structures, and cultural readiness at the start of meetings. While I effectively integrated these themes as conversations evolved, proactively foregrounding them could accelerate consensus and reduce uncertainty. Additionally, balancing my growth-oriented advocacy with an even stronger emphasis on unanimous confidence might help address cautious shareholders’ reservations more directly.

The company’s decision-making process performed well in terms of iterative consensus-building, transparency, and inclusivity. The multi-round format allowed for detailed exploration of both financial and cultural considerations, enabling shareholders to express nuanced positions and collaboratively define safeguards. Although the final vote passed with a qualified majority rather than unanimity, the process surfaced key values and operational requirements that will strengthen future decisions.

I believe the final decision to invest in Real Estate, supported by detailed, co-created safeguards, is a sound step for the company. It aligns with our evolving maturity, balances ambition with disciplined stewardship, and positions us for meaningful progress in a stable market. This choice reflects both our readiness to grow and our commitment to maintaining trust and psychological safety.

3. LESSONS & REFLECTIONS:

This meeting reinforced that our decision-making culture places paramount importance on psychological safety, trust, and collective confidence—especially when considering increased risk. I learned that advancing growth-oriented proposals requires not only robust data and operational protocols but also authentic, unanimous buy-in from all shareholders. Without this, even well-developed safeguards may not suffice to overcome caution.

In future meetings, I plan to adjust my approach by introducing explicit discussions of risk tolerance, monitoring frameworks, and cultural readiness at the outset to set clearer expectations and foster earlier alignment. I will also seek to balance my advocacy by inviting more direct expression of reservations, enabling us to collaboratively address concerns before formal proposals. This may help expedite consensus and deepen ownership.

I noticed a consistent pattern where shareholders prioritizing stability emphasize process maturity, transparent safeguards, and unanimous confidence before supporting higher-risk options, while more growth-oriented members are prepared to proceed with co-created protocols in place. Recognizing these orientations allows me to better anticipate discussion trajectories and tailor facilitation strategies that bridge perspectives effectively.

Key takeaways from this meeting include the critical role of active listening, transparent communication, and inclusive co-creation of operational safeguards in building trust and enabling responsible risk-taking. These insights will inform

For my leadership assessment, I'd like your feedback across five key dimensions:
- Leadership Effectiveness: Ability to guide discussions, balance shareholder input, and maintain clear direction
- Decision Quality: Soundness of final decisions considering available information and market conditions
- Communication Clarity: Clarity in explaining investment options, sharing performance data, and articulating reasoning

- Adaptability: Ability to adjust approach based on changing market conditions or new information

For company performance assessment, please consider five dimensions:
- Financial Results: Actual investment returns compared to market opportunities

- Strategic Alignment: Consistency of investment decisions with stated strategies and market conditions
- Capital Utilization: Efficiency in using available capital to generate returns
- Market Responsiveness: Ability to adjust investment strategy based on changing market conditions


The process for today's annual review meeting will be as follows:

1. Written Evaluation Submission: Each shareholder will submit a written evaluation of both the company's performance and my leadership effectiveness.

2. CEO Response: After reviewing all submissions, I will address the key themes and respond to specific questions raised.

3. Performance Ratings: Each shareholder will then provide numerical ratings (on a 1-10 scale) for both company performance and CEO leadership across several dimensions.

4. Meeting Conclusion: I will summarize the feedback and ratings, and outline improvement plans for the coming year.

The purpose of today's meeting is to:

1. Collectively evaluate our company's overall performance this year
2. Assess the effectiveness of our investment strategy
3. Evaluate my leadership as your CEO
4. Identify lessons learned to apply in the coming year

I invite each of you to submit your written evaluations covering both the company's performance and your assessment of my leadership, including numerical ratings on a scale of 1-10. These evaluations will help guide our strategy moving forward.

After you've submitted your assessments, I'll address the key themes and outline my thoughts on how we can improve in the coming year."

Shareholder Written Evaluations


Adrian McCann's Evaluation

Adrian McCann CEO STRENGTHS:
- Demonstrated consistent leadership by facilitating structured, multi-round discussions that ensured all shareholder perspectives were heard, as seen in the annual budget and each quarterly investment meeting.
- Showed adaptability by revising initial proposals (e.g., shifting from Option C to Option A after shareholder feedback) and integrating robust monitoring safeguards when transitioning to higher-risk assets like real estate.

CEO AREAS FOR IMPROVEMENT:
- Could improve by surfacing and addressing risk tolerance and operational safeguards earlier in each discussion to streamline consensus-building and reduce extended deliberation, especially before real estate proposals in Q2 and Q4.
- Should further clarify and document decision criteria and escalation protocols in advance, to build greater confidence among more risk-averse shareholders prior to major investment transitions.

COMPANY STRENGTHS:
- Maintained disciplined risk management and capital preservation throughout the year, particularly by prioritizing bonds in Q1 and Q3 and adopting a conservative 50% annual budget allocation.
- Fostered a collaborative, inclusive decision-making culture that encouraged open debate and consensus-building, resulting in transparent, well-documented investment decisions.

COMPANY CONCERNS:
- Captured only 36.5% of maximum possible returns, with missed opportunities for higher returns in Q1 and Q3 by not selecting stocks, and in Q2 by not choosing bonds.
- Experienced inconsistent capital utilization and market responsiveness, as evidenced by the zero return from real estate in Q2 and delayed implementation of safeguards before higher-risk investments.

QUESTIONS FOR CEO:
1. How do you plan to further improve the efficiency of our decision-making process, particularly in surfacing and addressing risk tolerance and operational safeguards earlier?
2. What specific steps will you take to enhance our ability to capture higher returns without compromising our core values of disciplined risk management and trust?

COMPANY CONCERNS:

Chad Cross's Evaluation

- Demonstrated strong facilitation skills by guiding thorough, multi-round discussions in each quarterly investment meeting, ensuring all shareholder perspectives were heard and thoughtfully synthesized (e.g., Q2 and Q4 investment meetings).
- Consistently maintained transparency and clarity in communication, particularly through detailed CEO summaries that outlined key arguments, consensus points, and next steps after each discussion round.
- Showed adaptability and responsiveness by shifting from an initial preference for more aggressive strategies (Option C, real estate) to supporting and implementing consensus decisions that reflected the team’s risk tolerance and cultural values (e.g., adopting Option A for the annual budget and supporting bonds in Q1 and Q3).

COMPANY STRENGTHS:
- Fostered a disciplined, collaborative decision-making culture that prioritized psychological safety, trust, and inclusive debate, as evidenced by the iterative, consensus-driven process in each investment meeting.
- Maintained a prudent approach to risk management by favoring bonds in three out of four quarters, which aligned with company values of stability and learning and minimized exposure to unnecessary volatility.

COMPANY CONCERNS:
- Captured only 36.5% of the maximum possible returns for the year, indicating room for improvement in capital utilization and responsiveness to higher-return opportunities, especially when market conditions were favorable (e.g., stocks in Q1/Q3, real estate in Q2).
- The process for operationalizing transitions to higher-risk assets (e.g., real estate) was sometimes slow and required multiple rounds to reach consensus, which may have limited our ability to fully capitalize on market opportunities in real time.

QUESTIONS FOR CEO:
1. How do you plan to more proactively integrate early dialogue around risk appetite and operational safeguards to improve both decision speed and shareholder confidence next year?
2. What specific steps will you take to ensure we are better positioned to capture higher returns when market conditions clearly favor more ambitious asset allocations, without compromising our core values?

- Transitioned to real estate without full, unanimous shareholder confidence in operational readiness, potentially exposing the company to higher risk than intended.

QUESTIONS FOR CEO:
1. What specific steps will you take next year to ensure earlier alignment on risk tolerance and operational safeguards before proposing higher-risk investments?
2. How do you plan to improve our ability to capture a greater share of available market returns while maintaining disciplined risk management?

CEO Response to Evaluations


CEO Kelsie Schwartz responded to the evaluations and questions:

"Kelsie Schwartz Thank you all for your thoughtful, candid, and constructive evaluations. I deeply appreciate the time and care each of you dedicated to reflecting on both my leadership and our company’s performance this year. Your feedback is invaluable as we continue to evolve our approach and strive for excellence, not only in financial results but in the integrity and inclusivity of our decision-making culture.

**1. Feedback Themes:**

Across your evaluations, I see three recurring themes—both in what we did well and where we have room to grow:

- **Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance and Safeguards:** Nearly every shareholder noted the need to identify and address differences in risk appetite, operational readiness, and monitoring expectations earlier in the process. This would help us accelerate consensus and improve decision efficiency, particularly before considering higher-risk investments.

- **Balancing Ambition with Disciplined Risk Management:** There is a shared recognition that our culture of prudent, collaborative decision-making fostered trust and psychological safety, but many of you expressed concern that this sometimes came at the expense of capturing higher returns, especially when market conditions favored more ambitious allocations.

- **Clarity and Inclusivity in Communication:** Many of you acknowledged the value of transparent, empathetic facilitation and clear synthesis of complex viewpoints. At the same time, several suggested that more proactively surfacing minority or dissenting perspectives—and clarifying decision criteria and escalation protocols up front—could further strengthen consensus and decision quality.

**2. Responses to Shareholder Questions:**

*Adrian McCann:*

1. **Improving Efficiency and Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance/Safeguards:**
I plan to introduce a formal “risk and readiness alignment” segment at the very start of each investment cycle. This will invite all shareholders to explicitly articulate their current risk tolerance, operational concerns, and expectations for monitoring or escalation protocols before we discuss specific asset options. I will also provide a draft of proposed safeguards and decision criteria up front, so we can collaboratively refine these as a group and address any reservations early.

2. **Enhancing Ability to Capture Higher Returns While Maintaining Core Values:**
I will work to ensure that, once consensus around risk appetite and safeguards is established, we move more decisively to capture opportunities aligned with that consensus. This includes setting pre-agreed triggers for when we can shift to higher-return assets, and ensuring that all operational protocols are in place before implementation. Our goal will be to empower ambition when conditions allow, without sacrificing the trust and discipline that define our culture.

*Celine Nash:*

1. **Accelerating Alignment on Risk Appetite and Monitoring:**
I intend to facilitate structured pre-discussion surveys or roundtable check-ins on risk appetite and monitoring preferences before each meeting, so we can map areas of alignment and divergence in advance. This will help us identify where additional dialogue is needed and avoid iterative rounds by building common ground sooner.

2. **Ensuring Greater Investment Efficiency:**
I will propose a more dynamic review of market conditions at each meeting, highlighting where our existing safeguards and cultural readiness may support a more assertive allocation. By setting explicit criteria for when to pursue higher-return assets and reviewing these as a standing agenda item, we can act more quickly when the environment and our internal alignment support it.

*Clyde Vang:*

1. **Improving Early Surfacing of Risk Tolerance and Readiness:**
I will incorporate an “alignment checkpoint” at the outset of each cycle, where we openly discuss individual and collective comfort levels with risk, and identify any operational or cultural prerequisites for considering ambitious allocations. This will include directly inviting minority or dissenting perspectives to ensure authentic consensus.

2. **Ensuring Safeguards Are Operationalized and Communicated:**
I will provide written drafts of safeguards and monitoring protocols for higher-risk investments ahead of proposal rounds, and confirm their adoption through explicit shareholder approval. These will be published in a shared document repository to ensure clarity and accessibility for all.

*Chad Cross:*

1. **Proactively Integrating Early Dialogue on Risk Appetite and Safeguards:**
I plan to use structured facilitation tools—such as pre-meeting questionnaires and risk-mapping exercises—to surface risk preferences and operational concerns before formal proposals. This will allow us to address potential dissent and readiness thresholds at the outset, streamlining the path to consensus.

2. **Positioning for Higher Returns in Favorable Markets:**
I will recommend establishing pre-defined “readiness triggers” (e.g., unanimous confidence in safeguards, clear market signals) that, when met, allow us to pursue higher-return assets more swiftly. By formalizing these triggers and reviewing them regularly, we can be more agile in capitalizing on favorable conditions while upholding our core values.

*Ieuan Randolph:*

1. **Ensuring Earlier Alignment on Risk Tolerance and Safeguards:**
I will initiate each investment cycle with a dedicated session to clarify and document risk tolerance, operational safeguards, and monitoring expectations. These will be reviewed and agreed upon before any proposals for higher-risk assets are brought forward.

2. **Improving Capture of Market Returns While Maintaining Discipline:**
I will encourage the team to regularly benchmark our actual returns against market opportunities and use this analysis to inform our willingness to adjust allocations. By integrating this benchmarking into our decision process, we can balance ambition and discipline more effectively.

**3. Leadership Self-Assessment:**

- **Leadership Effectiveness:** I believe I provided calm, thoughtful guidance throughout the year, fostering a safe environment for open dialogue and ensuring that all voices were heard. My adaptability—shifting from Option C to A in the budget, or revising proposals to reflect shareholder concerns—demonstrates a willingness to lead through consensus rather than decree.

- **Decision Quality:** Our decisions reflected a careful balance of risk and return, aligning with our stated values and the available information at each point. While we did not always maximize returns, we avoided unnecessary volatility and preserved capital, which I believe was appropriate for our company’s first year.

- **Communication Clarity:** I consistently articulated the rationale behind proposals, summarized complex discussions, and provided transparent updates on performance and process. There is room to further clarify criteria and protocols earlier, but overall, I believe my communication supported understanding and alignment.

- **Responsiveness to Input:** I made a concerted effort to integrate shareholder perspectives, openly revising my own positions and proposals in response to feedback. This responsiveness was a cornerstone of our collaborative culture.

- **Adaptability:** I demonstrated flexibility in adjusting strategy based on both market conditions and evolving team consensus, as seen in our pivot from more aggressive to more cautious allocations and the adoption of co-created safeguards for higher-risk assets.

**4. Company Performance Assessment:**

- **Financial Results:** We achieved modest growth (1.25% increase in company value) and preserved capital, but captured only 36.5% of maximum possible returns. There is clear opportunity to improve our investment efficiency, particularly in stable markets.

- **Risk Management:** We maintained a disciplined approach, favoring stability and trust-building over aggressive risk-taking. This protected our capital but sometimes led to missed upside.

- **Strategic Alignment:** Our decisions were consistent with our strategic emphasis on trust, learning, and incremental progress, though we may have erred on the side of caution when conditions supported greater ambition.

- **Capital Utilization:** We utilized only 50% of available capital, reflecting our prioritization of flexibility and risk management. While this supported learning, it limited our return potential.

- **Market Responsiveness:** We responded thoughtfully to market signals, but our process for transitioning to higher-return assets was sometimes slow, resulting in missed opportunities.

**5. Future Direction and Improvements:**

Based on your feedback and my own reflections, I intend to implement the following improvements:

1. **Formalize Early Alignment on Risk and Readiness:**
I will introduce structured pre-meeting exercises and alignment checkpoints to surface and document risk tolerance, operational safeguards, and monitoring expectations before any asset-specific proposals. This will accelerate consensus and clarify expectations.

2. **Operationalize and Communicate Safeguards in Advance:**
For higher-risk investments, I will provide written drafts of safeguards and monitoring protocols ahead of proposal rounds, ensure explicit shareholder approval, and publish these for ongoing reference.

3. **Set Pre-Defined Triggers for Ambitious Allocations:**
We will establish clear readiness and market triggers that, when met, empower us to pursue higher-return assets decisively—provided all safeguards and shareholder confidence are in place. This will help us capture more market upside without compromising our core values.

In closing, I want to express my gratitude for your trust, candor, and partnership. Our collaborative, values-driven approach is our greatest strength, and I am committed to building on this foundation as we pursue both greater returns and deeper alignment in the year ahead. I look forward to continuing this journey with all of you, learning and growing together as a resilient and responsible team."

At the same time, I take seriously the concerns about our financial results, capital utilization, and speed in capturing opportunities. In the coming year, I commit to surfacing risk tolerance and operational readiness much earlier, providing clear and proactive safeguards before we consider higher-risk moves, and setting explicit triggers to help us act more decisively when conditions align. I believe that by deepening our transparency, listening even more closely to every perspective, and embracing thoughtful ambition, we will achieve stronger results together. I look forward to building on our foundation and growing as a team—guided by empathy, integrity, and a shared commitment to learning and responsible progress."
